Abstract: Training parameterized quantum circuits requires optimization methods that account for two distinct structures: the information geometry of quantum states and the matrix or tensor structure induced by circuit parameterization. We introduce Quantum Natural Muon (QNM), a spectral natural-gradient framework that applies polar normalization to circuit-semantic parameter blocks after damped quantum Fisher preconditioning. This ordering is essential: quantum Fisher preconditioning and blockwise polar normalization do not commute in general, so QNM is not an order-free composition of quantum natural gradient (QNG) and Muon. We show that Frobenius-matched polar mixing preserves active singular subspaces while raising a spectral floor and improving active conditioning, and that the resulting direction remains a controlled perturbation of QNG. For its distance-aware realization (DA-QNM), we further establish alignment, descent, and nonconvex stationarity guarantees for the normalized update, together with perturbation bounds under gradient and QFIM estimation error. Experiments on variational quantum eigensolving, weighted QAOA-MaxCut, and supervised quantum classification isolate the roles of quantum geometry, circuit-semantic spectral structure, and radius control. The strongest gains arise when the spectral blocks match the circuit structure, supporting the central principle that quantum-state and structured spectral geometries provide complementary information for variational quantum optimization.

</details>

Abstract: Characterizing data center-grid interactions under rapid load fluctuations requires electromagnetic transient (EMT) models that capture fast converter and control dynamics. This paper develops a modular multi-fidelity EMT model of data center loads in PSCAD. The modular design supports flexible assembly of subsystem models for different power supply configurations. The multi-fidelity design incorporates switching models, average-converter models, and reduced-order models to balance simulation accuracy and computational efficiency according to analysis needs. The model also provides a systematic controller tuning procedure and supports control mode switching. Comparative simulations demonstrate how this data center load model responds to both grid-side and load-side disturbances and assess the accuracy and computational efficiency of models with three fidelity levels. The model is publicly available on GitHub.

</details>

Abstract: This paper presents a method for designing servo-controllers that enforce operational constraints on multi-input multi-output linear time-invariant dynamical systems. The proposed method introduces a control augmentation that complements a baseline controller to ensure forward invariance of state/output trajectories within prescribed box constraints. The derivations of the control design are based on the Nagumo Theorem, the Comparison Lemma, and principles from min-norm optimal control akin to the theory of Control Barrier Functions. Importantly, an explicit piecewise-linear feedback control law is derived, which provides continuous enforcement of constraints without online optimization, enabling rigorous stability and robustness analysis via classical linear systems theory. Extensions to handle estimation errors in output feedback and integral action in proportional-integral control are provided. Theoretical results are validated through flight control simulation studies that demonstrate input/output constraint satisfaction, robustness to disturbances, and closed-loop stability. The proposed method offers a mathematically rigorous, practical, and analyzable alternative to common heuristic or optimization-intensive constrained control strategies.

</details>

Abstract: In this work, we investigate how sample covariance obtained from frequency-response functions (FRFs) can be exploited to improve performance of data-driven frequency-domain predictive control (FreePC) in the presence of colored measurement noise. In case of periodic multisine excitation, this covariance is obtained directly from repeated periods and provides a frequency-dependent measure of uncertainty without requiring an additional parametric noise model. To incorporate this information into both indirect and direct predictive control, we develop a frequency-domain subspace predictive controller (FD-SPC) and establish its equivalence to the direct FreePC formulation in the noiseless setting. Regularization terms for both formulations are then motivated by expected prediction-error criteria and constructed from the sample covariance of the FRF estimates. Across all tested noise models and data sizes, regularization consistently reduces the median realized control cost relative to the corresponding controller without covariance regularization, with reductions of up to 35%.

</details>

Abstract: This paper presents an equivalent circuit model (ECM) for dynamic traffic modeling by exploiting analogies between electrical and transportation variables. Vehicle concentration is represented by capacitor voltage, while traffic flow is modeled using controllable current sources. The proposed formulation is shown to preserve the conservation and sending/receiving relationships of the spatially discretized Lighthill Whitham Richards traffic model. The framework is extended to restricted inflow, traffic signals, and two road merging and diverging scenarios. Validation against microscopic Simulation of Urban MObility (SUMO) simulations, involving traffic signals, two-road merging, and single-road diverging scenarios, demonstrates that the proposed ECM successfully reproduces the principal macroscopic traffic dynamics, including queue formation, backward congestion propagation, vehicle accumulation, saturation, and subsequent traffic recovery.

</details>

Abstract: An operator splitting method is developed for computing operating points of nonlinear resistive networks. Our behavioral formulation separates the device laws from Kirchhoff's laws and casts simulation as a zero-finding problem. Branch port resistances then provide a diagonal preconditioner for the relaxed Peaceman-Rachford iteration in scattering variables. Each iteration requires solving independent scalar equations and a linear system with a fixed weighted Laplacian matrix. Convergence is established for maximal monotone device laws when an operating point exists. Contractive branch reflections also yield a linear convergence bound independent of network topology. Numerical experiments on crossbar arrays illustrate the computational advantages.

</details>

Abstract: The property of real eventual exponential positivity guarantees the state trajectories of a linear time-invariant system to evolve asymptotically to right half of the complex-plane [1]. It has applications in the characterization of herdability and reachability of states in networked systems. Networks with complex-valued edge-weights arise in AC power systems and opinion dynamics. Recent work has introduced real eventual exponential positive (rEEP) Laplacians, for which the trajectories of the associated network flow eventually converge to consensus. We establish further properties of rEEP Laplacians and their pseudoinverses. Firstly, we show that the rEEP property for complex-valued matrices exhibits invariance under specific rotational transformations. Secondly, we establish that the property of rEEP is preserved under the MoorePenrose pseudoinverse operation, highlighting its robustness under algebraic manipulations. These results apply to effective impedance computation in AC networks and to consensus in multi-agent systems. Finally, we provide numerical examples to validate our theoretical results.

</details>

Abstract: We study the joint protection of a networked control system (NCS) against eavesdropping and data integrity attacks over a lossy wireless channel. The eavesdropper intercepts the transmitted sensor measurements and control inputs to reconstruct the system state. In contrast, the integrity attacker in this study replaces the sensor measurements with fabricated data to drive the system to undesired operating conditions. Although these two threats are typically studied separately, a wireless NCS should be protected against both simultaneously, and a defence against one affects the other. We employ an observation-encoding scheme that renders the eavesdropper's state-estimation error covariance unbounded under packet drops, and show that transmission energy governs how long this error remains bounded. In particular, lower transmission energy improves confidentiality. For integrity protection, we combine stochastic physical watermarking with a CUSUM-based sequential change detector. The proposed attack detection scheme accounts for intermittent communication and encoded measurements. The detection delay is governed by the Kullback--Leibler divergence (KLD) between the pre- and post-attack distributions of the test signal, and we show that a higher transmission energy increases the KLD and thereby reduces the detection delay. Furthermore, we derive analytical expressions for the eavesdropper's estimation error and the worst-case expected KLD, providing guidelines for selecting transmission energy to balance confidentiality and integrity protection and characterising the privacy--security trade-off. Finally, we run numerical simulations to validate our theoretical findings and demonstrate the privacy--security trade-off in practical scenarios.

</details>

Abstract: Practical networked robotic systems rarely implement ideal consensus interactions exactly, since local actuation constraints, sensing biases, and heterogeneous interaction maps may shift the input-output relations used by distributed controllers. Rather than focusing solely on whether consensus occurs, such imperfections can fundamentally alter the structure of the asymptotic collective behavior, typically yielding global or clustered velocity alignment, while exact static agreement arises only in special cases. This paper develops a shifted-interaction framework for analyzing collective alignment under imperfect actuation and sensing. Shifted functions provide a compact representation of local actuator and sensor maps, allowing actuation constraints and sensing biases to be represented by horizontally and vertically shifted monotone interactions. The framework distinguishes nodal shifted actuation from edge-wise shifted sensing. For shifted actuation, a Lyapunov analysis establishes global velocity alignment under compatible actuation ranges, including bounded, non-invertible, and deadzone-type maps. For shifted sensing, a maximum-principle argument and a paired-sensitivity condition establish convergence to velocity clusters over edges with infinite accumulated sensitivity; uniform sensing sensitivity yields exponential global velocity alignment. In the linear case, closed-form expressions characterize asymptotic behavior and identify actuation as a special case of sensing. Simulations illustrate static, globally aligned, and clustered velocity-aligned behaviors.

</details>

Abstract: Uncrewed aerial vehicle (UAV)-assisted mobile edge computing (MEC) is a promising technology, leveraging the advantages of UAVs including flexible deployment, wide coverage, and reliable wireless communication. In this paper, we study the task reassignment and offloading problem in a multi-UAV cooperative operating environment, and consider the real occurrence that the computing tasks arrive more frequently than UAVs make flight decisions. In addition, we also take into account the position offset of the UAV during flight and the unavailability of the UAV due to external interference. We propose a two-timescale optimization framework, where the trajectory of the UAV is carried out on a large timescale, while the task reassignment and computing resource allocation are jointly optimized on a small timescale. The core objective is to maximize the long-term average system gain, which takes both energy consumption and task delay into account. To address the UAV trajectory optimization problem under large timescale constraints, we propose a constrained multi-agent deep reinforcement learning algorithm. For decision-making within small timescale, to jointly optimize task offloading decisions and computing resource allocation, we integrate convex optimization with a heuristic algorithm. Finally, we conduct extensive simulations to evaluate the proposed algorithms against the baseline, achieving a 24% reduction in latency and a 28% reduction in total system cost.

</details>

Abstract: The growing demand for on-orbit servicing and constellation management in low Earth orbit requires autonomous and reliable low-thrust rendezvous. Traditional numerical methods struggle with the long-horizon nature of multi-revolution maneuvers under perturbations, while standard deep reinforcement learning (DRL) methods are limited by low simulation throughput and poor sample efficiency. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a massively parallel DRL guidance framework. A fully tensorized, GPU-resident orbital propagator is developed to integrate $J_2$-perturbed orbital dynamics and policy optimization within a zero-copy pipeline, achieving up to a 67-fold increase in simulation throughput over conventional CPU baselines. In addition, the rendezvous problem is formulated in modified equinoctial elements and trained with a progressive curriculum learning strategy to improve convergence and generalization across increasingly challenging rendezvous scenarios. Simulations show that the proposed framework can learn robust rendezvous trajectories for complex multi-revolution rendezvous over a broad range of orbital configurations. Additional evaluations under a high-fidelity model and eclipse-induced thrust interruptions further demonstrate the robustness of the learned policy under practical mission constraints. Moreover, the trajectories generated by the proposed framework can be used as effective initial guesses for optimization-based trajectory solvers, improving the convergence of high-precision trajectory optimization. The method offers a scalable approach for real-time low-thrust guidance and trajectory optimization.

Abstract: Networked multi-agent systems coordinating in adversarial environments face compound cyber threats that alternate between false-data injection (FDI), denial-of-service (DoS), and stealthy decoy perturbations designed to mimic benign operational anomalies. Because the switching policy is latent and the plant dynamics evolve continuously, deterministic threshold detectors struggle to isolate deception without causing excessive false alarms. This paper presents an online, data-driven framework that couples distributed continuous time unknown input observers (UIOs) with a topology-aware Hidden Markov Model (HMM) for real-time attack detection and classification under unknown adversarial strategies. By decoupling FDI signals from augmented node states, distributed observers deployed across the network generate local augmented residuals that are discretized into emission sequences. The HMM embeds the underlying directed communication graph into the off-diagonal blocks of its transition probability matrix, explicitly capturing inter-agent attack propagation. Both transition and observation probabilities are updated recursively online via an Expectation-Maximization (EM) scheme, adapting the classifier to evolving threat behaviours without prior offline calibration. Numerical simulations on a directed network demonstrate that incorporating graph topology into the latent transition dynamics resolves the severe classification ambiguity between nominal and decoy modes, outperforming decoupled agent-independent decoders while restricting observer deployment to a sparse subset of nodes.

</details>

Abstract: Power system dynamics involve multiple timescales, from electromagnetic transients of the network and inverter-based resources to electromechanical oscillations of synchronous generators and quasi-steady-state evolution of the operating point. Simulating these dynamics with a detailed electromagnetic transient model is computationally prohibitive, whereas existing methods reduce the model to focus on a single timescale and lose accuracy when dynamics of different timescales interact. This paper proposes a nested heterogeneous multiscale framework for highly efficient multi-timescale simulation of a power system represented by one unified detailed model. Rather than varying only the integration step, the framework adaptively shifts the simulation resolution among three nested levels so that the dynamics of interest at each stage are always captured, with the slower dynamics estimated on the fly from short detailed simulations at controlled accuracy. Theoretical analysis proves the stability of long steps during quasi-steady-state evolution and quantifies the attainable speedup. On a modified IEEE 39-bus system with frequency regulation and load restoration, the framework accurately reproduces the dynamics of all timescales and runs 15 to 18 times faster than conventional simulation of the detailed model. The accuracy and speedup of the proposed method are further verified on a 390-bus system, demonstrating its scalability to large power systems.

</details>

Abstract: Learning MPC requires large datasets generated by repeatedly solving MPC problems. For Nonlinear MPC (NMPC), dataset generation is computationally expensive because feasible initial states are difficult to sample efficiently. The feasible set is often non-convex or even disconnected, and many randomly sampled states are infeasible, leading to wasted optimization solves and unnecessary computational effort. In this work, we propose a data-generation framework for learning NMPC policies that combines active-set exploration with projection-based sampling. First, a bilevel formulation samples activity weights to promote solutions associated with different active sets while preserving optimality for the original NMPC problem; second, a simpler slack-based formulation uses weighted slack variables to target different active sets at the single level. These reference solutions are then used to construct local linearized feasible sets, from which candidate initial states are sampled and projected through the nonlinear NMPC problem to recover feasible and locally optimal training samples. Our approach is up to $37\times$ faster than baseline uniform sampling with $99\%$ acceptance rate, while maintaining a representative coverage of the feasible set.

</details>

Abstract: Electric vehicle (EV) charging stations are increasingly operated with an aggregate power limit below the sum of their port ratings, and require scheduling algorithms to divide the available power among the connected EVs. Charging standards permit a current that is either zero or at least a minimum rate, typically 6 A, which makes this allocation problem non-convex. Existing methods either solve a mixed-integer program with a commercial solver, which can be computationally expensive, adds software dependencies, and incurs licensing costs, or apply heuristics without optimality guarantees. This paper proposes Bisect-Index-Choose (BIC), a fast and solver-free scheduling algorithm that computes a globally optimal allocation among EVs under mild assumptions on their utilities. For $n$ EVs, BIC runs in $O(n \log n)$ time when all EVs share one maximum rate and in $O(n^2)$ time for two, times a logarithmic factor in the numerical precision. In tests with up to 1000 EVs, BIC attains global optimality and is up to three orders of magnitude faster than the solver. On real-world charging data, it needs less capacity and delivers more energy than existing heuristics. We implement BIC on a low-cost microcontroller and show that it solves instances with 1000 EVs in less than 90 ms.

</details>

Abstract: Currently, the high-voltage multi-terminal DC (MTDC) grids face two major challenges. First, fast rise rate and lack of zero-crossing point in DC-side fault currents render AC circuit breakers inoperable. Second, DC line currents are passively determined by terminal DC voltages and line resistances, which inherently limits the capability of changing individual line currents for essential grid services such as power flow management and overloading prevention. To address these two limitations, DC circuit breakers (DCCBs) and DC power flow controllers (DCPFCs) have been developed. The conventional DCCBs are two-port devices installed in series with DC lines at their terminals in an MTDC converter substation, so that any fault current flowing through the line can be rapidly cleared. Additionally, a DCPFC can be installed at line terminals to adjust terminal voltages and power exchanges among adjacent lines. As a result, for a substation that connects to multiple lines, it needs to host multiple DCCBs and a DCPFC, which significantly increases the cost and footprint of both the substation and the MTDC grid. To this end, this work proposes an integrated equipment combining multiple DCCBs and a DCPFC, called the interline multiport DCCB with DCPFC, which can reduce the number of components and enhance the economic feasibility of MTDC networks. This paper presents the topology, operation principles, and component selection procedures of the proposed equipment, and compares it with the state-of-the-art technologies. Electromagnetic transient (EMT) simulation results validate the fault interruption and power flow control performance of the interline multiport DCCB with PFC.

</details>

Abstract: Learning-based approaches to network-constrained unit commitment (NCUC) usually still rely on an optimization solver to repair or complete their predictions, which limits the speedup they can deliver. This paper proposes FALCON-UC, a feasibility-aware graph learning framework that predicts commitment and dispatch jointly and produces final schedules without solving a mixed-integer program at inference time. It combines an LSTM--GraphSAGE predictor over a fully connected generator graph, a differentiable correction that embeds commitment-dependent dispatch bounds, power balance, and PTDF-based branch flows in training, and deterministic commitment, dispatch, and ramp-coupled reserve repairs at inference. On the 2383-bus system, FALCON-UC achieves $99.09\%$ commitment accuracy and a $0.4350\%$ mean absolute cost gap with all evaluated constraints within tolerance at $0.0562$ s per instance, a $2.27\times10^{3}$ speedup over the solver. MTLR R-SCUC-FL is slightly more accurate but still needs an optimization solver to complete each schedule, which takes $262.2$ s per instance, $4.67\times10^{3}$ times the FALCON-UC runtime. A label-only baseline without repair violates the balance and branch limits on every 2383-bus test instance, and FALCON-UC also meets all evaluated constraint tolerances on a 1354-bus system.

</details>

Abstract: Learning-based safety certification offers a promising route for verifying complex dynamical systems when explicit models are unavailable, but existing data-driven methods often scale poorly: they treat the system as a black box, rely on dense discretizations or Lipschitz bounds, and consequently suffer from sample complexity that grows exponentially with dimension. This paper shows that a common structural property of dynamical systems--monotonicity--can be used to break this curse of dimensionality. We develop a data-driven framework for robust safety verification and safe controller synthesis for unknown monotone systems using only simulator queries. Our main theoretical result proves that, for monotone systems with upper-closed unsafe sets, safety is completely characterized by the existence of monotone inductive barrier certificates. This structure reduces certificate verification over continuous state spaces to localized boundary checks over finitely many cells. To learn such certificates, we introduce Max-Monotone Networks, a monotone neural architecture with universal approximation guarantees for monotone functions. We then propose a training and refinement algorithm that, upon successful termination, returns formally valid neural barrier certificates and, under mild partition assumptions, requires only $O(n)$ simulator queries. Across high-dimensional benchmarks, including oscillator networks with up to $13{,}659$ states and traffic networks with $1{,}000$ states, our method certifies safety in regimes where prior approaches either do not converge or exceed memory limits. These results demonstrate that exploiting order structure can make neural safety certification both scalable and formally sound.

</details>

Abstract: Inference providers size their accelerator fleets for the interactive peak and sell spare capacity through fixed discounts, such as batch tiers at half price. However, inference differs from conventional cloud capacity in four respects: capacity depends on the latency tier through batching, moving weights between models is slow and costly, spot demand can wait until its deadline, and power can bind. In this paper, we develop a framework for dynamic capacity allocation and pricing of AI inference on a shared accelerator fleet. We consider a provider that serves several models under a power cap and sells each model in an interactive tier with a speed promise and in a spot tier for preemptible work with a deadline. In each period, the provider chooses how many replicas of each model to keep loaded and what spot price to quote. We show that one interactive token displaces a fixed number $ρ_m>1$ of spot tokens, and that the optimal spot price lies midway between the interactive price and the effective cost of capacity in the deadline window, which is close to the prevailing half price only when the fleet is slack. We further show that a spot tier raises the optimal reliability of the interactive tier, and that placement should follow persistent demand shifts while prices absorb transients. Building on these results, we design a model predictive controller based on a linear program with explicit approximation bounds, which solves each period in milliseconds. Numerical experiments on a simulated week with a measured daily demand profile, a flash crowd, and a grid curtailment show that the controller raises profit by 7.4% over fixed half-price practice, captures 93% of the gain available with perfect forecasts, keeps the interactive promise through both events, and leaves spot buyers better off.

Abstract: In this letter, we consider trajectory tracking control and inertial parameter estimation of a quadrotor UAV whose center of mass (CoM) does not coincide with its geometric center. Onboard payloads like an IMU, a camera, or a battery are not necessarily placed at its geometric center. Abstract: Chip performance is limited by a shared bottleneck: the core produces heat faster than it can be moved out. We study the hardest case, RF power GaN HEMTs, whose hotspots exceed 5 kW/cm^2, and propose a horizontal near-junction active cooling topology built on electron entropy transport. Classical on-chip thermoelectric cooling faces a contest: Peltier absorption grows linearly with current, Joule heating with its square. In a vertical pillar, its own conduction resistance locks about half the Joule heat back toward the cold end, capping net cooling. From the Onsager framework we show the origin of this asymmetry: Peltier absorption localizes at the interface where the Seebeck coefficient jumps, whereas Joule heating fills the volume. The topology exploits this: a thermoelectric thin film lies flat on a diamond substrate beside the device drain (the electron exit), so bulk Joule heat leaves vertically into the substrate. A lateral fin model gives a closed form for the recirculation factor gamma: the vertical pillar yields gamma = 1, while the horizontal film yields gamma_max = sqrt(2) L_z/L_x, about 0.03 for L_z = 10 um, L_x = 500 um. The decoupling scales the net cooling ceiling and the critical runaway current by roughly 1/gamma. At equal resistance, the same film lifts maximum cooling from 13 mW to 500 mW, and the critical current from 0.65 A to 24 A. A time-constant and linear stability analysis with temperature-sensitive parameters defines the working window and safety margins for microsecond pulse cooling. The architecture leaves the device structure untouched; the principle, absorb at an interface and extract bulk heat vertically, applies to any chip with a hot core and offers a reproducible design path for near-junction thermal management.

</details>

Abstract: We study the least-cost co-expansion of power distribution networks and public charging ports to accommodate the growth of electric vehicles (EVs). Our approach, MAC+, exploits the flexibility of charging along each EV's itinerary and coordinates it across the fleet to reduce the peak violations of each feeder's hosting capacity and port capacity. This coordination is an extremely large, spatio-temporally coupled optimization problem with binary plug-in variables. We solve it by iterated price response (IPR), which repeatedly posts a two-tier tariff on feeder load and port occupancy, lets millions of EVs best-respond in parallel, and certifies the optimality gap at every iteration. We develop a custom exact dynamic-programming algorithm that solves each EV's best-response problem, a mixed-integer quadratic program, about 20 times faster than off-the-shelf solvers. The case study covers the San Francisco Bay Area at 30% EV adoption, with 1.8 million EVs on week-long itineraries and 1,303 feeder service regions, each with a utility-published hosting capacity and a port capacity set by its existing public ports. On this instance, IPR reaches a certified gap below 10% in a few hours. MAC+ saves about $15 billion of the upgrade investment that unmanaged charging would require. It also needs over 90% less investment than charging that is fully coordinated but flexible only within each session, and over 80% less than charging that is flexible along the itinerary but uncoordinated.

Abstract: Classical optimal control is used to design a separate controller for each plant. This paper asks a different question. Can one learned policy serve a whole family of partially observed, noisy linear systems? We show that one transformer can. The policy is trained to imitate optimal LQG control on a heterogeneous family of Linear Time-Invariant (LTI) systems with Multiple Inputs and Multiple Outputs (MIMO), whose dimensions, cost matrices, and noise levels all vary. A shared representation with system-wise standardization, zero-padding, masking, and an encoding of the cost and noise covariances lets one set of weights control every member of the family. We characterize the relation between the optimal LQG policy (represented as a dynamic state-space model) and a static transformer acting on a finite window of measurements. We then express the closed-loop excess cost of any causal policy as the energy of its deviation from the optimal action. Finally, we evaluate the approach on 28 simulated systems. The trained policy reaches the optimal cost to within a median gap of $0.4\%$ for seen systems. For unseen systems, a light fine-tuning step brings the median gap to $1.0\%$.

Abstract: Large-scale integration of inverter-based resources (IBRs) is widely known to cause subsynchronous oscillations. This paper investigates a distinct class of oscillations that occur in the electromechanical frequency range (EFR), referred to as EFOs, and are dominated by the DC voltage control (DVC) loops of grid-following IBRs rather than synchronous generator-like controls or phase-locked loops (PLLs). Although open-loop oscillation modes of DVC loops are subsynchronous, the corresponding closed-loop dominant oscillation mode related to DVC can shift downward into the EFR without retuning the control parameters. This frequency shift is caused by network-mediated interactions among the IBRs, which introduce a negative synchronous coefficient into the DVC oscillation loop. This synchronous coefficient reduction effect (SCRE) becomes stronger as the IBR plant scale increases and/or as the minimum eigenvalue of the network admittance matrix decreases. Furthermore, a complex vector-based stability analysis method is proposed, and a stability criterion for DVC-EFOs is derived. Finally, the analytical conclusions are validated using a practical power system configuration implemented in Simulink. The SCRE differs from traditional explanations, in which the open-loop oscillation modes are already located in the EFR, and provides new insight into DVC-dominated oscillations in the EFR for integration of IBR plants.

</details>

Abstract: Offset-free tracking is a key objective of any control algorithm. For predictive controllers with linear models, it is well understood that rejecting asymptotically-constant disturbances necessitates augmenting the prediction model with $p$ integrating states, where $p$ is the number of measured outputs. This can be achieved in model predictive control (MPC) by using a velocity form or a combination of disturbance model and observer. In data-driven predictive control (DPC), implicit incorporation of integrating states using the velocity form has been successfully adopted to obtain offset-free tracking; however, formulations based on an explicit disturbance model and observer have not yet been explored. In this paper, we first study how the integrating states are incorporated into the data-based representation used in DPC, translating the requirements of $p$ integrating states into the required modifications of the DPC formulation. We then propose a novel offset-free DPC formulation that incorporates an explicit constant output-disturbance model and an output disturbance observer. Numerical examples show that this formulation has similar disturbance-rejection capabilities to the velocity-form DPC, but using a smaller optimisation problem.

</details>

Abstract: Autonomous vehicles track planned trajectories close to obstacles. Unknown disturbances make them deviate from these trajectories. With a bound on the tracking error that disturbances can cause, a planned trajectory can be certified as safe before execution. Existing set-based methods bound the tracking error by one invariant ellipsoid for the whole trajectory or by intervals, which are both conservative. For aerial and space vehicles, the tracking error evolves on a matrix Lie group with nonlinear dynamics. However, for group-affine systems, the error dynamics are linear in Lie algebra coordinates when the vehicle receives the inputs of the reference. The control input and the disturbance enter through the inverse right Jacobian, a matrix that depends on the error. We propagate the linear part of the error dynamics with zonotopes and enclose the effect of the inverse right Jacobian on the disturbance in closed form. The resulting tubes are tight and provably contain all reachable tracking errors in continuous time. When some error components have no input, the inverse right Jacobian leaves unmatched nonlinear terms in the closed loop. None of the tested methods certifies such a closed loop. Hybrid coordinates make these terms matched, so that our controller cancels every nonlinear term that does not contain the disturbance. Our tubes give tighter bounds than the ellipsoid method in simulations of a planar aircraft and a fully actuated multirotor and in real flights of a quadrotor, where the tube contains every measured attitude error. Under larger disturbances and the same feedback gain, the ellipsoid method yields no valid ellipsoid, whereas our tubes remain valid.

</details>

Abstract: In this paper, we propose an input design framework for the safe online excitation and identification of uncertain linear systems without requiring informative initial data. The objective is to actively excite the system and collect sufficiently informative input-output data while maintaining safe operation and satisfactory control performance. Achieving this objective is challenging due to the potential conflict between the excitation required for learning and the safety and performance requirements, particularly when limited prior knowledge of the system is available. Inspired by the dual control principle, the proposed scheme balances exploitation for control performance with exploration for model improvement. A Bayesian learning approach is employed to update the model uncertainty online, starting from a conservative initial uncertainty set. As informative data are collected, the uncertainty set contracts, enabling progressively less conservative operation. To reduce the online computational burden, the resulting program is reformulated into a computationally tractable form. Furthermore, an online ellipsoidal terminal set with non-increasing conservatism is designed to guarantee the recursive feasibility of the proposed scheme. Numerical experiments are conducted to demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed framework, particularly in terms of uncertainty reduction and control performance.

</details>

Abstract: Extremely large-scale multiple-input multiple-output (XL-MIMO) arrays are hard to deploy in virtualized radio access networks, because the fronthaul data rate grows with the number of antennas when the radio unit forwards every antenna sample. We propose a two-timescale receiver that allocates the per-user combining ranks under the fronthaul stream budget from the channel statistics. The radio unit applies the long-timescale transform, after which the distributed unit applies a minimum mean-squared-error receiver in the reduced dimension. For disjoint visibility regions, we prove that the reduced-dimension receiver decouples across users. We characterize the sum-rate loss to second order and show that the greedy allocation, which we call saturating capture, minimizes its leading term. In simulations, the allocation gains over a uniform-rank baseline, matches a budget-trimmed threshold rule at a small number of users and exceeds it as the array and the user count grow. The gain grows with the heterogeneity of the per-user eigenvalue profiles and vanishes for identical profiles. Under out-of-cell interference offset by at least half a window width, the gain survives with clean covariances, which window-restricted estimation keeps for disjoint windows.

Abstract: This paper investigates the coexistence of two low Earth orbit (LEO) satellite communication systems sharing the same frequency band: a primary system with regulatory precedence over the spectrum and a secondary system that must not cause excessive interference to the primary system's ground users. This spectrum sharing paradigm was recently formalized by the United States Federal Communications Commission (FCC) through short-term and long-term protection criteria that limit primary-link unavailability and throughput degradation due to interference inflicted by the secondary system. To facilitate coexistence, we propose a satellite selection mechanism that associates the secondary system's satellites with clusters of ground cells at each handover to maximize secondary-user throughput while abiding by these regulatory constraints. Our mechanism enforces both constraints through a cumulative assessment that combines past primary-link performance with predicted performance over the upcoming handover interval. Simulation results show that our proposed method achieves up to 31% higher secondary-user throughput than prior work that imposes interference-to-noise ratio (INR) constraints, even when its constraints are tuned to satisfy the FCC's throughput-based protection criteria.

Abstract: Spatial multiplexing in dense low Earth orbit (LEO) satellite downlinks is hindered by two bottlenecks. First, line-of-sight (LoS)-dominant propagation from a high orbital altitude places the users in a narrow angular region, which limits the spatial resolution achievable by a single satellite. Second, the visible satellites cause severe inter-satellite interference. Both effects call for joint link association and precoding, because the association decides which users are served together and which streams interfere. However, conventional block coordinate descent with hard association updates suffers from premature turning-off, where an inactive link loses precoder power and cannot be reactivated in subsequent iterations. To overcome this limitation, we propose a soft-then-hard update scheme that relaxes the association into continuous weights via iterative scaling, keeping candidate links active. Annealing progressively concentrates the association weights as the precoders are updated, after which a single hard matching determines the serving links. We further extend this framework to the LEO space-time channel, where distinct Doppler shifts provide user separability beyond angular geometry. Numerical results show that, over the space-time channel, the proposed design outperforms the baselines while effectively exploiting this separability.

Abstract: Physical-scene reconstruction through visual sensing and channel knowledge map (CKM) reconstruction through wireless sensing have attracted growing research interest but have largely evolved independently within the computer vision and wireless communications communities, respectively. When applied to the same physical environment, the two sensing modalities provide complementary observations. Visual sensing captures detailed scene geometry and appearance but is constrained by occlusion and poor lighting. Wireless sensing can provide information beyond visual line of sight regardless of lighting conditions, but its measurements are often sparse and difficult to associate with scene structures. These complementary strengths motivate a unified multimodal approach to physical-scene and CKM reconstruction. This paper provides an overview of such unified environment reconstruction through wireless and visual sensing. We first compare the capabilities and limitations of the two modalities and discuss potential applications that benefit from jointly reconstructing both targets. We then review two complementary research directions, namely wireless-assisted physical-scene reconstruction and vision-assisted CKM reconstruction, highlighting how each modality supports the reconstruction task associated with the other. Building on these perspectives, we discuss design principles for a joint representation that supports both targets and identify mutual refinement, in which each modality helps improve the reconstruction associated with the other. Finally, we discuss open challenges and future research directions, offering insights into developing site-specific environment models for sixth-generation (6G) and beyond networks.

Abstract: Channel tracking in mobile terahertz (THz) near-field extremely large-scale multiple-input multiple-output (XL-MIMO) systems is challenging because spherical-wave propagation couples angle and range, leading to rapid and nonlinear channel evolution under sparse pilot observations. Existing approaches typically estimate pilot-local channel or position snapshots before applying temporal tracking or smoothing, making them vulnerable to information loss during snapshot formation. To break this bottleneck, we propose a paradigm shift from tracking discrete snapshots to estimating a holistic geometric entity, namely, one shared continuous UE trajectory over the tracking window. Specifically, we parameterize the trajectory using a low-dimensional cubic B-spline representation and directly couple the per-pilot profile likelihoods through this shared trajectory before any pilot-local position estimate is finalized, thereby inducing continuous near-field channel evolution. We further characterize the efficient geometric information supplied by each pilot observation and its aggregation in the shared spline-coefficient space. Based on this formulation, we develop a regularized maximum a posteriori estimator and a quadratic-penalty lifted solver that separates parallel pilot-local geometric updates from a structured global spline update. The resulting global system is banded and, for a fixed spline degree, admits linear-complexity factorization, enabling an efficient structured implementation. Simulations demonstrate consistently improved tracking accuracy over representative temporally refined baselines in the tested medium-to-high-SNR regime, together with a favorable accuracy--runtime trade-off. These results highlight the benefit of coupling sparse pilot observations through a shared continuous trajectory rather than temporally processing independently estimated snapshots.

</details>

Abstract: Graph neural network (GNN) detectors achieve high accuracy in large multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) systems, but exchange messages between all pairs of transmitted symbols, so their complexity grows quadratically with the number of streams. We first propose a sparse GNN (SGNN) detector that restricts each symbol's messages to its most strongly coupled neighbors, so the message count grows linearly with the system size. However, pruning discards small couplings that jointly shape the weak channel directions, which limit detection accuracy. Even on the dense graph, learned messages capture these directions only partially. We therefore propose a sparse GNN with spectral virtual nodes (SVGNN), which filters the node states through a scaled regularized inverse of the channel Gram matrix to emphasize the weak directions. With two neighbors per symbol, SVGNN reduces the number of multiplications by a factor of 18 relative to the approximate message passing GNN (AMP-GNN) in a 64 x 64 MIMO system. Simulations with QPSK, 16-QAM, and 64-QAM over i.i.d. and correlated channels show that SVGNN matches or outperforms both AMP-GNN and expectation propagation, with gains of up to 3.2 dB, and lowers the error floors of AMP-GNN in several configurations. The virtual nodes improve AMP-GNN even without pruning, indicating that message passing alone, whether sparse or dense, cannot fully capture the weak directions.

Abstract: Accurate extraction of site-specific multipath propagation properties enables a wide range of wireless applications, including initial access, wideband channel reconstruction, and the generation of channel datasets for learning-based and continual-learning systems. Classical parametric channel-sounding algorithms such as RIMAX provide physically interpretable estimates, but their data-dependent model-order selection and iterative nonlinear refinement introduce substantial delay and computational cost. This paper proposes MPSi, a physics-guided neural framework that replaces these costly stages with a compact path-count network and a fixed-depth, deep-unfolded Levenberg-Marquardt refinement. MPSi estimates the complex gain, angle of arrival, and propagation delay of each multipath component while preserving the underlying physical channel model. The reconstructed channels also yield bit-error-rate performance close to the perfect-channel knowledge reference. These results position MPSi as a promising low-overhead framework for extracting multipath propagation properties in massive-MIMO systems and for generating cell-specific channel pseudo-labels for training when direct ground-truth measurements are unavailable.

Abstract: Direct-to-cell (DtC) satellites serve unmodified LTE phones today from low Earth orbit, where the satellite's motion creates Doppler shifts of tens of kHz. Since large offsets such as these cannot be compensated adequately by the user equipment (UE), the satellite pre-compensates the Doppler shift of each beam, but it can do so for only one reference point in the beam. A UE elsewhere in the beam still has to compensate a residual offset; previous field studies report residuals of up to 10 kHz, beyond the $\pm$7.5 kHz that the 15 kHz subcarrier spacing can resolve. In this paper we corroborate this effect with our own detailed measurements of Starlink transmissions and further correlate ineffective handover performance to inadequate residual offset compensation. We analyze the Doppler compensation problem with detailed standard-specific simulations and propose a compensation method at the UE, based on the location of the UE with respect to the beam reference point used for the Doppler compensation and the known satellite location, that improves the efficiency of handover. With UE cell-specific pre-compensation, the neighbors are identified, read correctly and handed over to in time rather than lost to radio-link failure.

Abstract: Channel knowledge map (CKM) and integrated sensing and communications (ISAC) have emerged as two key technologies for sixth-generation (6G) and beyond wireless networks, enabling a new paradigm toward environment-aware system design. CKMs provide static or stochastic wireless environment information a priori by learning location-dependent mappings from transceiver positions to channel knowledge of various types, whereas ISAC exploits wireless signals to sense environmental objects in real time. CKM and ISAC have primarily been studied separately in prior works, while their integration offers a promising approach for enhancing the environment awareness of 6G networks and further improving both sensing and communication capabilities. On the one hand, sensing can support CKM construction by providing location-tagged measurements and richer environmental observations, while also enabling real-time CKM refinement in dynamic environments. On the other hand, CKM can enhance sensing by offering prior environmental information for clutter mitigation, and CKM and sensing information can be jointly leveraged to improve communications. This article provides an overview of the complementary interactions between CKMs and ISAC, with particular emphasis on three design approaches: sensing-assisted CKM construction and refinement, CKM-assisted sensing, and joint CKM- and sensing-enhanced communications. We also discuss future research directions for achieving environment-aware wireless networks.

Abstract: Wireless foundation models learn transferable channel state information (CSI) representations across heterogeneous wireless systems. However, conventional grid-aligned representations couple feature sequence length to CSI dimensions, restricting the allocation of representation capacity to different propagation conditions and task requirements while increasing inference overhead. This paper proposes WiFo-R, a wireless foundation model that learns progressive channel representations with selectable capacity. A channel-query cross-attention encoder aggregates spatial, temporal, and spectral CSI information, while parameter-shared recurrent updates refine existing channel features and introduce additional latent tokens. Multi-mask, multi-depth self-supervised pretraining equips intermediate states with strong reconstruction capabilities, followed by task-aligned post-training for channel estimation and CSI feedback. WiFo-R supports temporal and frequency-domain channel prediction, channel estimation, and variable-rate CSI feedback, and transfers to localization and LoS/NLoS classification. Experiments across in-distribution and out-of-distribution channel configurations demonstrate accurate reconstruction, progressive performance improvements, and strong transferability. A single feedback encoder--decoder supports six payload lengths at fixed quantization precision, while cross-attention and selectable recurrent depth improve the accuracy--complexity trade-off for large-scale CSI observations.

Abstract: Anchor self-calibration estimates the positions of fixed nodes of an indoor radio positioning or localization system from observations among the nodes and to mobile devices. It replaces the survey of every anchor, which is costly and goes stale when anchors move. The UWB Anchor Self-Calibration Dataset (UWB-ASC) is a testing ground for such methods, recorded in one industrial hall with ultra-wideband (UWB) two-way ranging. It has three parts. The first is two measured deployments. In the line-of-sight (LOS) deployment, 10 anchors range with a walking tag tracked by motion capture. In the non-line-of-sight (NLOS) deployment, 12 nodes range among obstructions, and one of them roves over surveyed markers in 20 quasi-static sessions. Both deployments also hold static sessions, and a total station surveyed every fixed node. The second part is a seeded range simulator that draws synthetic ranges on the surveyed geometry with adjustable error components, from noise-free ranges to NLOS errors, for benchmarks at controlled error levels. Its 81 frozen draws in 5 conditions serve as the reference. The third part is a set of readers that convert the ranges among the static nodes of two public UWB datasets, TWR-CLOUD-Bern and selfloc, into the same format without redistribution. All parts share open Parquet and YAML files, in which topology views select the ranges for each class of calibration method. Raw recordings with rebuild scripts, quality reports, and field labels of the line of sight complete the dataset.

</details>

Abstract: The Karhunen-Loève transform of a window of a stationary autoregressive process of any order $p$ is described through its poles, extending the Algebraic Diversity framework, which selects a transform by the matched group. For simple roots every basis vector is a sum of at most $p$ sinusoids, at the frequencies where the spectrum equals the eigenvalue, plus modes decaying from the ends. The window covariance differs from a member of the class diagonalized by the type-II discrete cosine transform by a closed-form matrix of rank $2p$, exactly when no pole is zero, so the eigenvalues interlace spectrum samples on the cosine grid, and the coding-gain loss of the cosine transform is an exact function of that matrix with computable bounds: for the $8\times8$ Markov block of image coding at pole $0.95$ it is below 1.2 percent. By Kronecker's theorem the rank is at most $2\max(p,q)$ for rational spectra; a moving-average window departs in its Hankel corners, and an integrated (ARIMA) window departs by low rank from the type-VII sine class, exact for the random walk. The exact symmetry is generically the reflection and contains a wreath product for interleaved windows; the dihedral group of the even extension is matched approximately, the dominant pole's sign choosing cosine or sine. The two groups give the transform a fast form: a parity fold, half-length cosine cores and a cascade of at most $p$ first-order Cauchy stages, exactly $p$ for a minimal AR($p$) model. The first-order case recovers results obtained by other means.

</details>

Abstract: Semantic sensing enables integrated sensing and communication (ISAC) systems to acquire environmental information relevant to downstream tasks. Abstract: In this paper, we study a movable antenna (MA)-enhanced covert communication system, where a legitimate transmitter (named Alice) serves multiple legitimate users in the presence of multiple aerial wardens that attempt to detect the transmission from Alice. To ensure data covertness with imperfect knowledge of warden channel state information (CSI), we develop a unified robust covert transmission framework that incorporates bounded and statistical CSI error models. In particular, we aim to jointly optimize the transmit beamforming, the artificial noise (AN) covariance matrix, and MA positions at Alice to maximize the minimum weighted signal-to-interference-plus-noise ratio (SINR) among the legitimate users subject to data covertness constraints. The resulting optimization problem is intractable to solve optimally due to the strong coupling among the optimization variables and the random channel uncertainty. To deal with this issue, we propose a block coordinate descent (BCD)-based algorithm. Specifically, for the norm-bounded CSI error model, we first employ the S-procedure to convert the covertness constraints into a sequence of linear matrix inequalities (LMIs). Then, a successive convex approximation (SCA) procedure incorporating semidefinite relaxation (SDR) and bisection search techniques is proposed to solve the resulting problem. For the statistical CSI error model, the Bernstein-type inequality is employed to derive deterministic approximations of the probabilistic constraint in terms of LMIs and second-order cone (SOC) constraints that can be efficiently tackled. Numerical results are provided to demonstrate the effectiveness and robustness of the proposed algorithm in improving the performance of MA-enhanced covert communication systems.

</details>

Abstract: Wireless digital twins are essential for environment-aware 6G communications, motivating the investigation of frequency range 3 (FR3), an emerging mid-band regime offering a promising balance between bandwidth and propagation characteristics. Neural Radio Frequency (RF) fields provide continuous surrogates for site-specific radio propagation, but adapting a scene-specific field to a new environment can require substantial measurements and retraining. To this end, we propose the Latent-Adaptive Neural Multipath Field (LANMF), a scene-conditioned FR3 model that predicts path-resolved delays, complex channel impulse response (CIR) coefficients, and arrival and departure directions from transmitter-receiver coordinates. LANMF learns shared propagation representations across source environments and adapts to unseen scenes by optimizing only a 16-dimensional scene latent. Evaluated on six ray-traced indoor scenes at 7.5 GHz, latent-only adaptation yields improvements in prediction with just 5-10 target links (0.42-0.83% of available links). Compared with full-network fine-tuning, it updates only 16 persistent target parameters, reducing adaptation time by 28-30% and incremental peak GPU memory by 82-85%. While the proposed solution supports various wireless applications, we demonstrate its utility through beam-candidate reduction using a 64-beam codebook, retaining measurement-based final beam selection.

Abstract: Direct-to-device (D2D) satellite systems, particularly those based on 3GPP-compliant non-terrestrial networks (NTN), deliver connectivity to standard mobile phones and IoT devices using terrestrial International Mobile Telecommunications (IMT) spectrum. This raises the risk of harmful cross-border interference into incumbent terrestrial IMT networks, an issue considered under Agenda Item 1.13 of the ITU World Radiocommunication Conference 2027 (WRC-27). This paper presents an ITU-R-compliant assessment of aggregate downlink interference from two hypothetical D2D systems operating in Jordan and Iraq into terrestrial IMT user equipment (UE) in Saudi Arabia, operating in the 698-960 MHz band. Unlike prior studies that evaluate a few test points near a simplified ideal tri-border intersection, we distribute multiple victim UEs along realistic national borders within various strips at a distance of 0-40 km from the borders. We compare two satellite selection strategies: highest-elevation and random strategies. The CDF of the interference-to-noise ratio (I/N) together with the intra-system and inter-system aggregation factors are analyzed. Results show that the protection criterion is exceeded up to 88% of the time, requiring up to 27 dB of additional isolation, so coexistence is infeasible without interference mitigation. In general, random selection produces higher interference because low-elevation beams place victims closer to boresight. Two mitigation techniques are then evaluated. A border isolation zone of one cell diameter yields only a moderate improvement under both strategies, whereas a narrow-beam, deep-null phased-array pattern lowers the exceedance to about 40% irrespective of strategy, providing an important input for spectrum regulatory considerations.

</details>

Abstract: Conventional channel estimation for multiple-input multiple-output orthogonal frequency-division multiplexing (MIMO-OFDM) relies on the pilot interpolation without explicitly identifying the sparse delay-angle (DA) components. To exploit DA sparsity while maintaining backward compatibility with existing MIMO-OFDM systems, we propose a cross-domain channel estimation scheme for multiuser MIMO-OFDM (MU-MIMO-OFDM) systems. The scheme exploits the circular convolution structure in the DA domain to identify candidate DA pairs by correlating delay-domain observations with DA pilot responses. These pairs and the known pilots then define a time-frequency (TF) dictionary for refining the DA coefficients from TF observations through least-squares or sparsity-regularized fitting. The refined coefficients and candidate pairs reconstruct the aggregate channel across antennas and subcarriers aligning with the geometric channel model. Numerical results show approximately 10 dB and 7 dB lower normalized mean-square error than the conventional OFDM estimation schemes for integer and fractional delay and angle indices, respectively.

Abstract: Relative greedy weights were introduced by Li and Liu in [19] as a measure of information leakage in coordinated two-party wire-tap channels of type II when the adversary follows a greedy strategy. Their definition can be viewed as a relative version of the CEZ greedy weights, which are only locally greedy. In this paper, we revisit the concept of greediness and relative greediness introducing new measures, and we discuss other perhaps more relevant applications of relative greedy weights than the coordinated two-party wire-tap channel of type II. We pay special attention to bottom-up greedy weights, which correspond to an adversary with an ultimate greedy approach, and we present families of nested codes for which such weights are larger than the relative CEZ greedy weights.

Numerical results under near-field spherical-wave channel models confirm that the DU framework achieves sum-rate performance close to the AO scheme with substantially lower and nearly constant runtime, and that both schemes consistently outperform manifold optimization under discrete hardware constraints.

Abstract: This paper studies multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) free-space optical (FSO) quantum communication systems employing polarization-encoded discrete-variable quantum key distribution (DV-QKD), based on entanglement swapping, with the goal of extending secure communications beyond the reach of a direct link. The FSO channels are subject to atmospheric absorption, diffraction, beam wander, pointing errors, and turbulence-induced fading, while background noise and detector imperfections degrade the performance. An eavesdropper controlling the channels and the relay is also considered. To analyze the performance of the considered system, both hops MIMO channels are decomposed into parallel spatial eigenchannels via singular value decomposition (SVD), photon loss is modeled using Kraus operators, and a threshold-detector model is developed for the relay. Based on these models, closed-form per-eigenchannel expressions for the relay gain are derived, which are then used to obtain expressions for the quantum bit error rate (QBER) and the secret key rate (SKR). Numerical results demonstrate that entanglement swapping maintains a low QBER over longer distances than direct transmission, extending the communication range beyond the noise-limited cutoff of the direct link, at the cost of a lower SKR at short distances. The crossover distance beyond which entanglement swapping prevails is determined mainly by the background noise and the detector technology, and it is demonstrated to increase with MIMO configurations. Moreover, the reach gain is shown to be maximized for a midpoint relay. Finally, since each swapping event requires a single-pair emission from both sources, a near-deterministic entangled-photon source is required, which spontaneous parametric down-conversion sources do not provide at practical key rates, whereas MIMO substantially relaxes this requirement.

</details>

We also show that all our APN functions have the maximal possible algebraic degree $n(p-1)$. Finally, we exhibit both CCZ-equivalent and CCZ-inequivalent APN pairs with boomerang uniformity one at Hamming distance one.

Abstract: We study the robustness of two state-of-the-art BP-based qLDPC decoders, Relay-BP and beam search, designed for independent noise when applied to spatially correlated noise with unchanged single-mechanism error probabilities. We generate correlated errors with a hidden Gaussian Markov random field, which lets us control the strength and range of the correlation while keeping every marginal error probability exact. This setting complements correlation-aware decoding: it asks how existing decoders perform when their assumed joint error model is mismatched. Across multiple tested circuits and code instances that we simulated, we observe that correlations can change logical error rates by orders of magnitude, reduce the finite-size distance advantage, and alter the relative ranking of Relay-BP, beam search, and BP-OSD. On one of the two tested circuits, Relay-BP and beam search become worse than BP-OSD, and increasing relay memory or beam width does not compensate for the degradation. Configuration analysis indicates that correlation primarily increases the probability of trapping configurations that are known to challenge these decoders; at matched error weight, correlated errors still cause decoding failures several times more often than i.i.d. errors. Finally, supplying the decoders with the marginals conditioned on the hidden field recovers most of the lost performance in the tested settings identifying prior mismatch as a major contributor to performance degradation under correlation.

</details>

Abstract: Accurate first erroneous position (FEP) identification is key to improving the error-correction performance of successive cancellation flip (SCF) decoding. However, FEP identification remains challenging with a limited number of re-decoding attempts. To address this issue, we propose decision-boundary normalization (DBN) to improve FEP identification and develop a novel DBN-SCF decoding algorithm. DBN normalizes the magnitude of each decision log-likelihood ratio (LLR) by the standard deviation predicted by the Gaussian approximation (GA) for the corresponding bit-channel. This allows a single normalized threshold to be shared across bit-channels within each code configuration, giving it a consistent statistical meaning and avoiding per-channel calibration. DBN-SCF decoding selects candidate flip positions from the information set in the natural successive cancellation (SC) decoding order using a threshold, without sorting. Moreover, DBN-SCF decoding achieves better error-correction performance with a limited number of re-decoding attempts. It also requires lower average decoding complexity than existing SCF decoding methods at a comparable level of error-correction performance.

</details>

Abstract: Quantum synchronizable codes protect quantum information against Pauli errors and against misalignments of the block boundaries. Almost all known constructions use cyclic codes, because the channel shifts the transmitted block cyclically. We study what happens when the algebraic shift of the code differs from the physical one. When one uses a twisted padding, constacyclic codes synchronize at no cost, and they are the only polycyclic codes with this property for a linear padding. For the other polycyclic codes, we measure the cost of each misaligned position, and we show that the phase damage it causes can be corrected as erasures. Using annihilator duality, we extend the construction to quasi-polycyclic codes, in which the cost is confined to a single block. We prove that the usual quasi-cyclic families have a distance bound that does not grow with the length, and show that quasi-polycyclic codes over repeated-root trinomials overcome it, giving synchronizable codes with larger dimension and distance than the known synchronizable codes of the same length.

</details>

Abstract: We study the optimal sampling and transmission policies for a transmitter communicating with n gossiping receivers with the objective of tracking a source's status over time using a semantic freshness metric: Version Age of Information (VAoI). The transmitter observes the version changes in the source, and can acquire a new information version by paying a sampling cost. The receivers gossip, allowing information exchange among each other without direct transmission. Gossiping can save on transmission cost, but it may propagate stale version information, necessitating transmitter intervention. With imperfect communication links, we formulate an infinite horizon average cost Markov Decision Process (MDP) in order to jointly optimize receivers' VAoIs, sampling and transmission costs in a time slotted fully connected (FC) gossip network. We first solve the MDP using Relative Value Iteration (RVI) algorithm. Then, we prove that the sampling decision has a threshold structure in the transmitter VAoI. Further, under reliable gossiping links, we show that transmission and idling has an VAoI-difference threshold structure with minimum VAoI activation. Numerical results obtained using RVI show that the optimal policy consistently outperforms natural baselines. These results characterize how source sampling, direct transmission, and receiver gossip should be jointly coordinated for cost-aware semantic freshness in gossip networks.

</details>

Abstract: Near-field communications have emerged as a promising technology for future sixth-generation (6G) networks. However, designing energy-efficient hybrid beamfocusing schemes for multiuser (MU) systems remains challenging due to the non-convex quality-of-service (QoS) constraints and hardware limitations of hybrid architectures. In this paper, we investigate a hybrid massive multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) architecture for a near-field sub-THz MU downlink system employing a large uniform circular array (UCA) at the base station. A QoS-aware transmit power minimization problem is formulated for energy-efficient beamfocusing and addressed through a two-stage penalty-driven joint optimization (PDJO) framework, where auxiliary variables are introduced to reformulate the QoS constraints into second-order cone (SOC) constraints. Furthermore, a near-field polar-domain codebook is developed via joint angle- and distance-domain sampling. The formulated problem is then solved using a low-complexity simultaneous orthogonal matching pursuit (SOMP) algorithm under hardware and transmit power constraints. Abstract: An analytical and optimization framework for the joint design of communication and control (JDCC) in a center-fed pinching-antenna system (C-PASS) is proposed. The analysis captures the mutual interference between concurrently transmitted data and control streams during uplink state uploading and downlink command delivery. Accordingly, a throughput maximization problem is formulated to jointly optimize digital and pinching beamforming subject to control-cost constraints. To address this problem, an alternating optimization algorithm is developed based on constraint reformulation and resource decomposition. The proposed framework reveals how control requirements shape achievable data throughput, guiding the joint beamforming design. Abstract: It is well-known that one can recover a function $f$ bandlimited to $[-δ,δ]$ ( $0<δ<π$) from its finite sample data $f(j),-n\le j\le n$, with an approximation error exponentially decaying to zero as $n$ tends to infinity. Various regularized Shannon sampling formula have been proposed to achieve such exponential approximation. So far the best rate has been
  $$
  O\biggl(\frac1{\sqrt{n}}\exp(-(π-δ)n)\biggr).
  $$
  We establish that the optimal recovery algorithm in the worst-case scenario achieves an approximation error $ρ_{n,δ}$ satisfying
  $$
  c_* \frac1{\sqrt{n}}(\tan\fracδ4)^{2n+1/2}\le ρ_{n,δ}\le C^* \frac1{\sqrt{n}}(\tan\fracδ4)^{2n+1/2},
  \quad 0<δ\leqπ,\quad n\geq1,
  $$
  where $c_*,C^*$ are positive constants independent of $n$ and $δ$. This result identifies the best possible exponential rate to reconstruct bandlimited functions from two-sided finite oversampling data. In other words,
  $$
  ρ_{n,δ} \asymp \frac{\tan\fracδ4}{\sqrt{n}}\exp\bigl(-c_δ(π-δ)n\bigr),
  $$
  where $c_δ$ is strictly decreasing on $(0,π]$ and
  $$
  \lim_{δ\toπ^-}c_δ=1,\ \lim_{δ\to 0^+}c_δ=+\infty.
  $$
  We also make the new finding that one can also reconstruct $f$ bandlimited to $[-δ,δ]$ with exponentially decaying error from one-sided sample data $f(j)$, $1\le j\le n$, and the sharp rate of approximation error $ρ_{n,δ}'$ of the optimal algorithm is
  $$
  ρ_{n,δ}' \asymp \sqrt{δ/π}\sin^n(δ/2).
  $$

Abstract: Private local decoding imposes a stringent constraint on compression: the codeword bits used to recover a source symbol must reveal nothing about the remaining symbols beyond what the recovered symbol itself reveals. For memoryless sources, this constraint is known to be compatible with entropy-rate compression. We show that the same holds for any stationary ergodic source over a finite alphabet. Specifically, every rate above the entropy rate is achievable with exact privacy and error-free local decoding on a set of source sequences whose probability tends to one. In contrast, we exhibit non-ergodic sources for which private local decoding incurs a strictly positive rate penalty.

</details>

Abstract: A fixed finite binary Ising model has only finitely many admissible complex belief propagation fixed points, even at degenerate parameter values. Families of positive fixed points obtained by varying external fields can nevertheless have nontrivial topology. We study this distinction through the external-field projection. For a fixed field, we prove finiteness by bounding the pole orders of the magnetizations on a hypothetical complex solution curve. The generic degree on the support graph then bounds the total local multiplicity. A graph has a single smooth real line as its full positive response for every choice of positive couplings, base field, and strictly positive sweep direction exactly when it contains no two vertex-disjoint connected subgraphs of cycle rank at least two. Two such subgraphs suffice to create a circular component. For a plane obtained by varying two vertex fields, connected simple bipartite cubic graphs with one uniform coupling and a universal bound on the fixed fields realize every genus. The minimum number of vertices grows logarithmically in the genus, by a matching algebraic upper bound on Betti numbers. Finally, we certify the complete field discriminant of an eighteen-vertex product model and recover its torus component's genus from the counts of distinct roots on all field cells. The three main theorems, including the logarithmic size bounds, are formally verified in Lean 4 with Mathlib.

</details>

Abstract: Traditional communication resource allocation optimizes operational metrics such as rate, latency, energy efficiency, and reliability. Motivated by goal-oriented semantic communication, we develop a generalized allocation framework that transforms operational rewards through reference-dependent user utilities and evaluates them via expected utility, cumulative prospect theory (CPT), or related decision criteria. The formulation separates operational rewards, user-side valuations, and system-level decision criteria under uncertainty. We characterize utilities incorporating QoS/service reference levels, deficit sensitivity, post-reference saturation, and loss aversion. Weighted sum-reward maximization, proportional fairness, $α$-fairness, and stochastic expected utility maximization emerge as exact special cases, while soft QoS-aware allocation follows from a deficit-penalty generalization. For deterministic orthogonal systems, we derive utility-weighted marginal-reward KKT conditions characterizing global optimality under concave composite objectives. For nonorthogonal and stochastic systems, we develop successive convex approximation (SCA) algorithms and establish stationary-point guarantees under explicit smooth and composite nonsmooth assumptions, including a CPT branch-stationarity result under eventual nondegenerate ordering identification. For globally solved penalized problems, we derive an $O(1/λ)$ bound on excess aggregate soft-QoS deficit, and on the deficit itself whenever a QoS-feasible allocation exists. Additionally, we establish an $O(1/ε^2)$ outer iteration bound for the smooth fixed-step SCA best-response residual. Numerical examples demonstrate how reference points, marginal utility slopes, channel quality, and resource budgets influence active users and allocation policies.

</details>

This paper proposes a joint estimation method for link-specific large-scale fading coefficients (LSFCs) and a common Nakagami-$m$ shape parameter using power and downlink SINR measurements. Abstract: Bit-interleaved coded modulation (BICM) is widely used in multiple-input multiple-output (MIMO) wireless systems. However, separating MIMO data detection from channel decoding and only exchanging per-bit soft information discards correlation information among the bits within each transmit vector. We show that the resulting information loss limits the performance of practical one-shot receivers. Even though iterative detection and decoding (IDD) can recover most of this loss, IDD significantly increases receiver complexity and latency. In order to reduce this loss, we propose COrrelation-Informed Logits (COIL), a soft-output format that combines per-bit soft information with pairwise coupling logits for correlated bits. We show how to incorporate these coupling logits into a conventional belief-propagation-based channel decoder, which enables correlation-aware decoding in a one-shot receiver without IDD. We evaluate COIL in a MIMO-BICM system with a conventional low-density parity-check code. For a 4x4 MIMO orthogonal frequency-division multiplexing (OFDM) system, a one-shot linear minimum mean-square error (LMMSE) receiver with COIL outperforms a conventional one-shot unbiased LMMSE receiver by 1.3dB and operates within 0.8dB of four-iteration IDD, while requiring only a single pass through the data detector.

</details>
